69. else. Whole political parties turned to fill Jewish life with terror and insults. After the success of Hitler, who used the same methods, as we shall tell, they went further. They spoiled synagogues and Jewish cemeteries, insulted and beat up Jews on the streets, and stirred up riots and murders. The police and the courts refused to give the Jews real protection. They too were influenced by the selfish nationalism which insisted that the Jews were not really a part of the Polish nation, even though they had lived in Poland for over a thousand years. Soon the whole government was won over to an anti-Jewish program. 

The anti-Jewish parties enforced boycotts against Jewish merchants. They forced Jews to sit in special "ghetto benches" in the universities. They secured from the government laws which prohibited sh'chito, or the slaughtering of meat according to the Jewish custom. The government had practically announced that the Jews would be forced to leave Poland when, in 1939, Hitler turned on Poland and without a formal declaration of war, smashed it with his Blitzkrieg. 

In defense of Poland, Jews now fought side by side with non- Jews in courageous but hopeless resistance. How cruel and bitter was the scene in which Jews struggled and died, with patriotism equal to the most devoted Pole, to preserve the very land which was on the point of driving them out and robbing them of their native soil! Even on Rosh Ha-shono and Yom Kippur, bearded

Orthodox Jews dug trenches. They fought this way because they were loyal, as Jews have been in every land in which they have lived. They knew also that Hitler was an enemy even more cruel than their Polish attackers. We shall take up the story of what happened to Polish Jewry under Hitler later on. 

RUMANIA 

South of Poland, in the land of Rumania, we find another example of how the decline of Democracy brought suffering and disaster to our people in Europe. Here too the tragedy grew out of similar causes. The first of these was poverty. 

Rumania, a land of almost a million Jews in 1939, need not have been a poor country. It has enormous natural resources of oil, minerals, and grain. But, even worse than in Poland, this wealth was controlled by a few families. The energies of the 

Rumanian leaders were used up in murderous attempts to seize power from each other rather than in making the people prosperous and contented. So the Rumanian peasants, or ordinary farmers, were the poorest and most heavily in debt of any farm group in Europe, even though their land was the most fertile. 

When these people then complained to the government that they could not rise up out of their poverty, the leaders would put them off by saying, "Your trouble is the Jews, they own the businesses that are overcharging you and taking away your money. 

And the leaders were easily able to fool the people in this way because in Rumania most of the stores were owned by Jews.

J

70. 

The anti-Jewish movements claimed that by getting rid of the Jews they would open up many good jobs for the "true" Rumanians. 

It became "patriotic" to attack Jews, and the government itself refused protection to them. As early as 1923, when a Rumanian sheriff by the name of Manciu arrested the leaders of gangsters who had beaten up Jewish students at a University, the gangsters brought charges against Manciu, saying that he had arrested them falsely. A political leader by the name of Codreanu defended the gangsters and, right in the courtroom, shot Manciu to death. As he did it, he cried out that Manciu was a traitor to the country because he defended the Jews. So great was the popular support of Codreanu that at his trial he was declared innocent of murder and was set free. He then became a popular national hero. 

Codreanu's political party, the Iron Guard, was really a little army. 

Its battle-cry was to attack Jews. As the Iron

Guard grew in power, it became more violent and reckless. It specialized in riots, and in 1933 even went so far as to murder the Prime Minister, Ion Duca, because he insisted upon keeping order.

The Iron Guard and other anti-Semitic parties received money and help from Hitler, as he rose to power in Germany, because that is the kind of government Hitler believed in. Besides, if 

Rumania could be made anti-Semitic, it would side with Hitler and help him in his plans to control the world. Nazi spies and agents therefore helped to organize these gangster parties and to buy control of Rumanian newspapers to spread their propaganda against Jews, as well as against France and England, with whom Rumania had been an ally.

These efforts finally produced results. Boycotts were organized against Jewish merchants. Associations of doctors and lawyers began to ask their Jewish members to leave. Universities made life unpleasant for Jewish students. 

The government itself prepared laws to take jobs away from Jews. It did this especially through its "citizenship laws. 

In Rumania, just as in Poland, selfish nationalism also helped to destroy democratic ideals. As a result of World War I, Rumania had been given large new territories which included many peoples who were not Rumanians. In order to protect these people, the 

League of Nations made Rumania promise to make them citizens, equal to other Rumanians. The Rumanian government, however, did not keep its promise. Especially in the case of the half-million 

Jews who were added to Rumania, the government gradually deprived many of them of citizenship rights. This meant that they could not stay in Rumania because no one was permitted to employ them, and they could not go to another land because they were citizens of no country. The Rumanians had forgotten the word of God in the Bible: "One law shall there be to him that is home born, and unto the stranger that sojourneth among you." (Ex. 12:49)

The Jews of Rumania were terribly frightened. They did not know what to do. Protests from the democratic countries secured

FI I. 1.

71. for them permission to stay in Rumania a little longer. But their situation was really no better. This was their condition when World War II broke out,

HUNGARY 

The story of the large Jewish community of Hungary between the two World Wars, was very similar to that of Poland and of Rumania. Before World War I its Jews had been among the happiest and safest of all the Jews of Europe. But with the defeat of 

Hungary in World War I, the old strong government broke down, and the new ones that took power were not able to maintain themselves. 

One reign of terror followed another, with the last one directed particularly against Jews. As the country settled down and a certain amount of prosperity returned, the Jewish situation again became better. But toward the end of the 1920's, when business troubles and unemployment spread over Europe and America, and later, when Hitler came to power in Germany, the Jewish position became worse. Laws were introduced which allowed only a few 

Jews in every occupation and took away citizenship from all Jews who had gotten it since 1914. 

One must laugh at the fact that the man who introduced these laws, Premier Imredy, was later proved to be one-eighth Jewish, and therefore, had to suffer from the very evils which he had planned for others. Later, when Hungary became an ally of Hitler, the laws against the Jews became more oppressive than ever. 

OTHER COUNTRIES 

It was not merely in Poland, Rumania, and Hungary that the hopes for greater Democracy after World War I failed. Dictators likewise seized control in Italy, Austria, Lithuania, Latvia, and other lands of Central and Eastern Europe. They arose out of conditions like those in the lands which we have studied. And with their coming, the position of the Jewish citizens grew worse. 

Only in those countries which kept their democratic spirit, such as England, France, Czechoslovakia, Holland, Belgium, Denmark, and the like, could the Jews of Europe live happily during this period.

GERMANY AND THE RISE OF HITLER 

By now you have probably guessed that Hitler, the most dangerous and murderous attacker that the Jew has ever had, was not simply a bad man who happened to hate the Jews. For, you must be saying to yourselves, "Probably the same things that happened in Poland, Rumania, and Hungary must have happened also in Germany. 

These must have caused Hitler to come to power. And you are right.

In Germany, in fact, the conditions that destroyed Democracy could be seen even more clearly than anywhere else. One of these was nationalism in its most selfish form. Germany had been beaten in World War I. The peace treaty put the blame for the

72. war upon her. It took away some of her land in Europe and all of her colonies. It forced her to agree to pay an enormous sum (called reparations) to rebuild areas which she had destroyed. 

The Germans who considered themselves to be the greatest people on earth, could not believe they had really lost the war. Somebody must have cheated them out of it. The generals, especially, insisted that they fought hard and should have won. Somebody else must be to blame. "The Jews robbed us of our victory, the 

Nazis said. "They stabbed us in the back. While we were out there fighting they stayed home and stirred up trouble, and that is what caused us to lose." When the Germans told themselves this, they felt happier.

Of course, it was untrue. The Jews of Germany, some six hundred thousand in 1933, or less than one per cent of the total population, were a loyal part of the nation. They had fought and died with the same patriotism as any other group of Germans. Indeed, Baron Manfred Von Richthofen, one of Germany's greatest flying aces, was of Jewish origin. Another, Fritz Haber, invented a method of obtaining nitrogen, without which Germany could not have fought the war. But truth does not count when embittered men look for someone to blame.

Germany decided to get even. She would fight again and win, and show the world that she is still the greatest nation on earth. 

She took up her old slogan even more strongly than before: "Deutschland uber Alles (Germany above everything else). This hope made the people ready to accept another false idea which had been developed by European writers at the end of the last century: the idea that there are superior and inferior races on earth, and that the superior ones are meant to rule over the inferior ones. 

The "Nordics, the term they gave to the Christian people of Northern Europe, were said to be the greatest race on earth, and all the best culture was said to have come from them. Other races were declared only to have imitated them.

After the War, the Germans carried this notion further. 

They declared that the Germans are the highest race among the "Nordics," and therefore deserve to rule the world. Certain other groups such as the Poles and the Negroes, they maintained, deserve only to be slaves. The lowest of all, they said, is the Jewish people, which lies, steals, and oppresses others by its very nature, and destroys all that is pure and beautiful in the world. 

It therefore deserves to be destroyed. 

Here you have selfish nationalism in its most unreasonable form. Indeed, despite the fact that it was taught to the German people as a new truth, based upon "racial science," it was really a form of insanity.

How did the German people with the finest universities and the highest education on earth, come to accept such dangerous nonsense? The answer is to be found in the bitterness after

World War I which did not die out but became increasingly worse. 

The people were, of course, in a bad condition right after the

73. war. tions.

They had used up most of their wealth for guns and muni- They had lost two millions of their men, and the country was full of the wounded. Rickets, a disease caused by lack of milk and vitamins during the war, had left many of their children with bones that were weak and improperly formed. 

It is true that for a time after the war, wiser and more democratic leaders in the population took hold. They set up a 

Democracy for the first time in Germany. They drove out the 

Kaiser, and urged their people to realize that the military way of trying to rule over other peoples does not work. Instead, they said, let us forget about our defeat, buckle down to work, pay up the money demanded by the Allies, and build a strong new 

Germany by peaceful means.

The great new leaders worked hard at their task, and they probably would have succeeded but for one serious mistake; they failed to remove from places of power and from their offices the military class that had led the nation into its wars before. 

This class of generals, landowners and leaders of industry, refused to believe in a peaceful way of living. They thought of only one thing another war with a German victory. 

Even when this military class organized rebellions against the new Democracy, as they did in 1919 and in 1923, and murdered several of its leaders, the new government took no forceful action against them.

After the war, troubles began to overtake Germany. There was inflation in the country. This means that the prices of things go up and up, and the money that people have can thus buy less and less. The government was unable to check this and the inflation went so far that men would have to bring a wheelbarrow full of money to buy a loaf of bread. With this happening, people had to spend their savings just to buy food. Thousands of merchants had to go out of business because conditions were so upset. By the time the government was able to get control of things again, millions who had always been independent, fairly comfortable people, found themselves without businesses or savings. You can imagine their bitterness against their government and against their conquerors as they started out searching for some kind of a job, any kind, just to keep themselves alive. 

It was then that the anti-democratic party, which called itself the Nazi, or National Socialist party, gained greatly in strength. It attacked the Peace Treaty, the Allies, and their 

Own government for giving in to the other nations. In the midst of this they attacked the Jews. "The Jews are our misfortune. 

Everything was blamed upon them. "They robbed us of winning the war, they are to blame for the new German Democracy. One of its great leaders had, indeed, been a Jew, Walther Rathenau, who had been murdered by the Nazis in 1922.

In 1923, the Nazis tried revolution. They attempted to seize the government of the state of Bavaria. 

They were easily

.brid v ? 1 J. 97805 2N noweg 2 por 1

74. stopped, and Adolph Hitler, one of their leaders, was sent to prison. Here he wrote his book, Mein Kampf (My Battle), which became the bible of Nazism.

During the next few years, a real attempt was begun by the democratic nations to improve conditions in Germany. For there was fear that Communism or Nazism might take control. Plans were worked out to make the payment of German reparations easier. 

Huge sums of money were loaned to Germany to put her industries back on their feet. The German people became more comfortable. 

But in 1928 there began the world depression. Businesses everywhere were closed down, and men were thrown out of work, Banks were closed; trade between nations became less and less. 

Germany was hit especially hard. By 1932 the number of its unemployed reached almost eight million or between one-fourth and one-third of all the German workers. As conditions became worse, the Nazi party grew stronger, until it became the largest of the many political parties in Germany.

On January 30, 1933, Hitler was named Chancellor of the German Reich by the aged President Hindenburg, and a new election was set for March fifth. Hitler took steps to make sure that he would be elected. He took control of the police and prohibited all criticism of himself as Chancellor. His party then used a master trick. Six days before the election, it burned down the 

Reichstag, or the capitol building, and blamed it on the Communists. With this as an excuse, Hitler sent the police and Nazi troopers up and down the land, terrorizing the people and carrying off to prison the leaders of parties opposed to him. In this "free" election, Hitler won even more votes. But it was still not a majority. By keeping the Communist deputies out of the meeting, however, Hitler commanded a majority, who then declared that the German Democracy had come to an end and that Hitler would be dictator for the next four years. 

He set up

From then on, Hitler ruled with absolute power. concentration camps where the leaders who had opposed him were tortured to death. A secret police was established with spies and agents everywhere. Anyone overheard criticizing the Nazi movement in the slightest was beaten up or tortured and advised to "cooperate." All newspapers, books, magazines, and the radio were put in charge of a Minister of Propaganda, Joseph Goebbels. 

Every printed and spoken word now had to echo the ideas of the 

Nazi party.

One of these ideas was that the Jews must be destroyed. years the Nazi storm troopers had been marching to the song: "Proud waves the flag in the wind 

When swords with Jewish blood will run.

For 

Now that they had the power, they began to destroy the German Jewish community of 600,000 people.

75. 

The first step was that of terror. Among the hundreds of thousands put in torture chambers or concentration camps when Hitler took control, many were seized simply because they were Jews. Thousands of these were never seen alive again. 

The next step was to take away all opportunity for a Jew to make a living. On April 1, 1933, a nation-wide boycott against

Jewish stores was organized. Great signs, "Don't buy from Jews," were hung on Jewish stores, and Nazi storm-troopers stood in front keeping people out. Laws then were announced that took away the position of Jews employed in government service, schools, courts, or railroads. Soon associations of lawyers and doctors began to drive out their Jewish members. Jewish authors, composers, and actors were deprived of their positions. Many of the most famous of all German Jews began to flee from the land to which they had brought great honor. Among them were five winners of the Nobel prize, including Albert Einstein and Fritz Haber.

The next step came with the Nuremberg laws of 1935, which sought to bring shame on the Jewish people. The laws declared first that the Jews were no longer citizens; they therefore could no longer expect any rights or protection from the government. 

Secondly, Jews were forbidden to marry non-Jews, for this would destroy the "purity" of the true German, who is an "Aryan.

Finally, Jews were forbidden to raise the German flag or share in its national life in any way.

Propaganda of all sorts was used to lead the people to fear and despise the Jews. Some of it pretended to be reasonable. It was repeated that the Jews had too great a control over German life. Hitler, in June, 1933, declared that 62 per cent of government office-holders were Jews. (Actually, the Nazi government itself declared, in 1937, that the men dismissed from office because they were Jews had amounted to 1,984, or less than twotenths of one per cent of the government officials!) Out of about 250 Cabinet Ministers who guided the German Republic from 1918 to 1933, only two were Jews and four others were of Jewish descent. With similar falsehood the Jews were attacked as controlling the wealth of the country, the newspapers, medicine, law, music, and the like.

In some of these fields, Jews were, indeed, among the greatest men that Germany, or even the world had produced. Germany should have been thankful for their efforts, and for the glory that it brought her. Selfish nationalism however, made her jealous of them. 

Since the beginning of the 1900's, a forged document pretending to show that Jews plotted to control the world had been used by anti-Semites in every country. It is called "The Protocols of the Elders of Zion." It pretends to be the Minutes of an assembly of important Jews from many countries meeting secretly to rule the world.

Although proved to be a complete forgery by Philip Graves in

1921, and by several courts of law in Berlin (1923) and Berne,

Switzerland (1934), the document continued to have great effect in stirring up anti-Semitism. It shaped Hitler's thinking and led him to write many portions of his book, Mein Kampf. It was used by Goebbels to make his country look with suspicion upon anything that a Jew might do.

The argument that the Jews are Communists was used over and over again by Nazi propaganda, It declared that they had caused the revolution in Russia and were behind Communism in Germany. 

It was easy to point to several leaders in both countries who were of Jewish descent. Of course, it was nonsense to say that the Jews were both Communists and international bankers at the same time. But this did not bother the propagandists. Pretending to be the savior of Europe from "Atheistic Communism' the Nazis called to help them all who feared Communism, and led them to hate the Jews as its cause.

The fact was that of the fourteen Jewish representatives in the Reichstag just before Hitler took power, thirteen were of democratic parties, and only one was a Communist. that of the eighty-nine Communist representatives, only one was a Jew. But there is no need to keep repeating that this German propaganda was false. In his book, Hitler openly declared that he would use lies to gain his point. "Propaganda does not have to state the truth.....but must serve its own interests. In another place, he wrote that a lie is believed more and more when it is boldly repeated, and no matter how untrue, some of it always remains,

These lies were put not merely in the papers and books and on the radio, but in the schools as well. Children were taught to hate the Jew in every class, from their nursery rhymes, which pictured him as an ugly monster, to the highest classes on "Race Theory.

After five years of such teachings, the German people were ready to go farther in their anti-Jewish policy. Three hundred and fifty thousand Jews still remained in the country, broken and poor, but still there, primarily because they had no other land to which to flee. In September, 1938, the Nazis were filled with a new sense of power because they had been able to seize Czechoslovakia without a fight. They took 12,000 Jews of Polish descent living in Germany, and in the middle of the night shipped them off to Poland. Poland refused to admit five thousand of these. For weeks they were left in a "no-man's land" between the two boundaries, seeking protection from the cold and rain in the open fields and in stables. When Herschel Grynszpan learned in Paris that his aged parents had received such treatment, he seized a revolver and shot Ernst von Rath, an assistant in the 

German Embassy in Paris.

Two days later, von Rath died. That was the excuse the 

Nazis were waiting for. Early the next morning, November 10, 1938, organized bands of Nazi stormtroopers tore into every

C. PIS mi I.

77. 

Jewish community. They burned down the synagogues of Germany, Austria, Czechoslovakia, and Danzig 413 in all, including many of the most ancient and magnificent Jewish houses of worship in the world. They ripped out the Torahs and tore up the prayer books. They smashed into Jewish homes and stores, stealing what they wanted, breaking what they did not. They terrorized the women and children and threw 50,000 of the men into concentration camps. They then ordered the Jewish community to repair all the damage and to pay a fine of $400,000,000. This was simply a "legal" way to take away any wealth or property that might still be left to the Jews. By 1939, it was announced, every last Jewish business would be taken away.

The world was shocked, as were many German non-Jews themselves. But the Jews of Germany now knew that all hope was gone, that the Nazi promise to destroy them completely would be carried out. Jews now began to beg the Consuls of other nations to grant them permission to enter their lands, to save their lives. 

America did not change its immigration laws, but it did relax the orders that had almost stopped all immigration during the depression. It admitted 150,000 non-Jews and 125,000 Jews (mostly from lands under Hitler) during the four years 1938-41.

NAZI PROPAGANDA USED TO UNDERMINE OTHER NATIONS 

The Nazi attack upon the Jews was not limited only to their own country. They likewise sought to stir up all other countries of the world to do the same thing. Their purpose was clear: by awakening old prejudices, which most people have against folks of a different religion or race, they sought to unite the world under Hitler, as the great enemy of the Jews. Therefore, when 

Germany formed the Fascist alliance, known as the Axis, she insisted that all her allies adopt an anti-Semitic program. This she felt, would join the common people of all these lands in a single burning purpose. She forced anti-Jewish laws upon Mussolini, the Italian dictator, who had often laughed at Hitler's race theories and had promised the ancient and well-loved Jewry of Italy that they would never be mistreated as long as he would rule. She led Hungary and Bulgaria to make more severe their measures against our people. 

Even Japan was led to adopt this program. This teaches us something. It makes perfectly clear that anti-Semitism was not just an attack upon the Jews (for Japan has practically no Jews). 

Instead it was really a trick to divide and confuse the democratic powers,

The Nazis therefore filled every liberal land with their propaganda, Wherever possible, they helped to set up pro-German political parties with little Hitlers at their head: Sir Oswald

Mosley in England, Colonel de la Rocque in France, and Leon 

Degrelle in Belgium. 

They sought especially to win over America to anti-Semitism and thereby keep our powerful nation from entering the war and

rys

CT, 1 זי

78. winning it for the democracies - as we did in 1918. Dr. Josef 

Goebbels, Reich Minister of Propaganda declared: "Nothing will be easier than to produce bloody revolution in America. No nation has so many racial and social tensions. We shall be able to play upon many strings there. The main string he sought to use was prejudice against the Jews. Whole boat loads of pamphlets and books, printed in Germany, full of lies and cartoons against our people, were scattered throughout the land. 

Nazis were sent to this country to organize the German- American Bund, in which men pledged loyalty only to the Führer and promised to obey his representatives in this land. They drilled with Nazi uniforms and flags, and at every meeting repeated their hatred of the Jew.

More dangerous than the Bund however, were the hundreds of anti-Semitic organizations led by native Americans which the Nazis encouraged and supplied with propaganda. Among the leaders of these were William Dudley Pelley, organizer of the "Silver

Shirts and editor of a weekly magazine called Liberation; Rev. 

Gerald Winrod of Wichita, Kansas; and George Deatherage, chief of the Knights of the White Camellia, who had invited a retired U.S. Army Major General, George Mosely, to head a Fascist uprising against the government. 

But the cleverest and most powerful of them was Father

Charles E. Coughlin, a priest whose weekly radio broadcasts reached millions of people, and whose magazine, Social Justice, had a circulation of hundreds of thousands. In his speaking and writing he often repeated the very lies which Dr. Goebbels was spreading, sometimes using the same phrases. 

The hatred stirred up by Father Coughlin was soon turned into a new channel: street fighting - which the Nazis had used so well in undermining Democracy in Germany. Salesmen of Social 

Justice magazines were placed on the busiest street corners of New York City especially. Here they would shout anti-Jewish slogans and insults and provoke Jews who passed by. An organization called the Christian Front would hold open air meetings in parks and squares, condemning the President and threatening violence to the Jews.

Some violence did occur. In February, 1939, a Jewish man was stabbed by an anti-Semite in a subway station. In June, a high school boy was beaten up in Baltimore and a Jewish high school teacher was stabbed by a Social Justice vendor. 

During 1943, forty-three Jewish synagogues in New York City, Boston, Philadelphia, and other places, had windows smashed and swastikas printed over them. Jewish children were beaten up by gangs as they left Hebrew schools and synagogues. 

The Christian Front drew up a so-called "Christian Index of non-Jewish stores and urged people to boycott all others. 

Here was Hitlerism right in America.

79. 

One of the chief purposes of this campaign became clear when Germany invaded Poland in 1939 and the war began. Every one of the hate organizations now attacked England, in an effort to keep America from supporting the democratic nations. 

But on December 7, 1941, Japan treacherously attacked Pearl

Harbor. Then the nation rose as one man to the realization that 

Hirohito and Hitler were out to destroy Democracy everywhere. 

Many of the friends of Hitler tried to continue their newspapers even then, but after a time many of their attacks upon the government and upon President Roosevelt were stopped by Federal authorities as interfering with the war. 

Today, in the midst of war, most of the American Hitlerites have been stopped or driven underground. Anti-Semitic propaganda now comes mainly through German short-wave broadcasts and unsigned letters, jokes, and leaflets. But the little Hitlers are still meeting in private, making plans for new attacks, should they find new openings in the democratic wall of strength that is now being built.

THE FASCIST WAR AND THE MASSACRES 

In America, the coming of the war tended to check anti- Semitism, as something unpatriotic, But in Europe, the coming of the war brought new and unheard of tortures to our poeple. 

For when in September, 1939, Germany attacked Poland, she began a series of lightning conquests which put in her power millions of our people. Wherever her armies went they brought with them the idea that the Jews are to be destroyed. Germany had already spread her Nuremberg laws (see page 75) to Austria, which she swallowed up early in 1938, and to Czechoslovakia, which she took over late in that year. Now she applied these measures, and worse, to the half of Poland which she conquered in 1939. She did the same in Denmark and Norway, then in Holland, Belgium, Luxembourg, and France. 

With her sudden attack upon Russia in June, 1941, the Nazis smashed into the very heart of the lands in which most Jews lived in Eastern Europe. Here lived millions of Polish, Russian, Rumania, and Baltic Jews. Here the Nazi blitz swept into Vilna, called the "Jerusalem of Lithuania, Kishinev, whose eighty 11 thousand Jews outnumbered its non-Jewish inhabitants two to one. 

Bialystok with its Jewish majority, Kiev with 140,000 Jews, Minsk,

Odessa, and Lwow, with over one-fourth of their populations Jewish.

This was the same zone in which bitter fighting had occurred in World War I. In 1914, Russia had driven Jewish families out of their homes along the border to keep them from being friendly to the Germans. In 1941, German troops removed Jewish work-crews from the border lest they make friends with the Russians. The dates were reversed, the enemies reversed, only the suffering of the East European Jew remained the same.

80. 

By the winter of 1941, six million out of a total of sixteen million Jews of the entire world were in the power of the Nazis. 

Their methods of treating these unfortunate people grew more severe as the war continued. To the Nuremberg laws which insulted our people and took away their jobs, there were now added 

1) the Badge, 2) the Ghetto, and 3) Slave Labor. These were methods used in the Middle Ages which we have read of before (III, pp. 75-76).

But the methods worked out differently than in times past. 

In the Middle Ages, special badges which the Jews had to wear made non-Jews look with ridicule and hatred upon our people. 

Children made fun of them, rowdies threw stones at them and pushed them off the streets. Now, however, it was just the opposite. The badge brought the people to help the Jew, and thousands of non-Jews put on Jewish badges to show their sympathy with these unfortunate people. They did it also as the first public sign of their defiance of Hitlerism. 

When Hitler ordered the Jews to put on yellow arm bands with the Mogen Dovid (Star of David) upon them, so many Christians put them on in cities of Belgium and Holland that the order was withdrawn there. In Denmark, King Christian himself told the officials that if the Jews were forced to wear the badge, he and his royal household would also wear it. And he showed his defiance of Hitler further by attending a special service in his honor at the Copenhagen Synagogue.

Even within Germany, the anti-Semitic newspaper Der Sturmer complained that Jews were wearing their badges with "provoking dignity and pride," and that Germans were rising in street cars to give up seats to those who wore them. 

In France, dressmakers showed their contempt of Hitler by creating little embroidered flowers on dresses that looked surprisingly like Jewish stars. The Allied soldiers who landed at 

Dieppe were said to have been greeted by Frenchmen who wore the Mogen Dovid to show their loyalty to Democracy. Most striking was the action of Mgr. Chaptal, Bishop of Paris who, in September of 1942, put on the Mogen Dovid and paraded prominently in the streets to express his opposition to the Nazi program. The sign of Israel on a Catholic Bishop! Thus did the Mogen Dovid grow into a symbol, not merely for the Jews, but for all men, a symbol of liberation, and mercy and justice for all men. 

Here we see the power of God working through history. It transformed even the worst depths which our people has ever suffered into a spiritual victory. Even without their choosing, the 

Jewish people became more clearly than ever before, exactly what the prophet Isaiah had long ago declared it to be: a people whom the 

Lord has "Kept and set 42:6-7). 

For a light of the nations; 

To open the blind eyes,

To bring out the prisoners from the dungeon." (Isaiah

The 

The ghettos, too, were not like the medieval ghettos. 

Nazis were determined to make them horrible places in which to live. The crowding of Jews into the worst sections of the city, without enough food, without enough medicine or doctors, brought a tremendous death rate through starvation and diseases that spread like wildfire. The half million Jews in the Warsaw ghetto soon were dying at the rate of one out of every five each year. 

But the ghettos, likewise, gave the non-Jews a chance to show their opposition to Hitlerism. Polish Christians, who had scarcely enough food for themselves, threw food and flowers over the walls of the Warsaw ghetto. To keep up the Jews' courage, they smuggled in newspapers of the "underground" secret groups who were fighting Hitler. They also gave them guns and ammunition. We shall tell more of this later. 

At the same time, the strongest Jews were organized into labor companies which were really slave-crews. Of course, all 

Germans were in a sense in bondage, since the Dictator could order them to do any work that he set for them. They had no labor unions or representatives in the government to defend themselves. But the German was called a glorious worker and praised and given all the privileges and food the government could spare from the war effort. The Jewish worker, however, was degraded and mistreated like the lowest slave. He was given the hardest and most dangerous work: mining in poisonous sulphur pits, building fortifications near the front, and a railroad across the burning Sahara Desert. Jews were driven to their work without proper food and rest. Thousands died. The

Yet the badge and the ghetto and slave labor were not enough to satisfy the Nazi hatred. Angered by the fact that the expected quick victory was not being won, indeed might never be won, the Nazis determined to take it out on the Jews. In the summer of 1942, they adopted a program to set up extermination squads. 

These were especially trained squads who would jam hundreds of Jews together in freight cars, seal the doors shut and ship them off, often through freezing weather, to some point in Eastern Europe. When after the days of travel, the doors would be opened, most of the Jews would already have died a horrible death. rest would then either be confined in new ghettos or be marched off to woods where they would dig their own graves and be shot and allowed to fall in them. Sometimes the people were put into sealed trucks and exterminated with poison gas fumes. Other horrible and painful methods of extermination were devised which we shall not record. Future generations already have enough to look back upon in shame for the manner in which men of the twentieth century so wickedly disobeyed God's word: "Thou shalt not murder.

The reaction of Christians to these massacres was likewise different from that of the Middle Ages. Then only the few protested and the rough masses howled their approval. When, however, Jews were rounded up to be shipped away in Paris railway stations, the French people gathered around them and defied the

Nazis' orders by singing the "Marseillaise," their national anthem of freedom. When the Nazis seized control of unoccupied France as well, in the fall of 1942, Frenchmen hid Jews on their farms and helped them escape to Spain. In a conquered section of 

Russia, guerrilla fighters organized a chain of stations by which Jews were led at night by side roads back to Russia and freedom. 

In a certain area, fifteen hundred Jews were saved by this system which worked like the "Underground Railway" of the American Civil War. In 1943, when the Nazis reached out their hands to ship 

Danish Jews away for execution, the Danish police refused to obey the Nazi command to arrest them. The common people helped most of the Jews flee on tiny boats to Sweden. This country admitted them freely without any passports at all and took care of them. 

Sweden even refused to accept money from Jewish organizations to feed and house the refugees, saying that it was its religious duty as a Christian people to do that itself. 

JEWISH COURAGE 

The power of man's spirit was seen also in the actions of persecuted Israel, which showed amazing courage in resisting the Nazi steam-roller cruelty. The "pride and dignity" with which they wore their Jewish badges was something which their own newspapers constantly encouraged them to do. Their leaders served the people without thought of themselves even unto death. The great guiding men of German Jewry, Dr. Julius Seligsohn, Dr. Otto 

Hirsch, and Rabbi Leo Baeck, all could have escaped from Germany many times. But all felt that their place was with their people, to do whatever they could to help. The first two died in the tortures of the concentration camp. Rabbi Baeck stayed on and spent World War II suffering in a concentration camp in Czechoslovakia.

In the ghetto of Warsaw, its great leader, Adam Cherniakov, continued in office, month after month, as the representative of the Jews before the Germans. He hoped he could soften their anger in some way. When, however, they ordered him to begin to deport his people to what he knew meant death, he committed suicide rather than help this program. 

As the Jews of Warsaw realized that Germany meant to exterminate them, and as they saw their numbers growing fewer and fewer, they determined not to give up without a fight, even though they knew the struggle was hopeless. They would teach the Nazis the spirit of an unconquerable people. They turned the walls which made them outcasts into a fortress of strength. They used the arms smuggled in by Polish Christians of the "underground" movement and for three weeks they fought off all Nazis that sought to enter. The Nazis finally had to bring up tanks, planes and heavy artillery to crush the defiant fighters of the ghetto. The water supply had to be cut off and the ghetto burned down, building by building. The Nazis won. They knew however, that they had been in a fight and many would fight no more. 

Jewish tradition of fighting evil, even when armored with overwhelming power, scored another victory. The

83. 

In spite of all this courage, however, Hitler had made terrible progress toward the destruction of our people. By 1944, it was estimated that he had put to death over three million Jews - one out of every five in the world. And his work went forward, with three million more still in his power. Only the victory of the United Nations would be able to stop his extermination squads. Jews throughout the world joined with all free people in heroic labors to win that victory as speedily as possible.

VIII. 

HOW DID THE JEWISH PEOPLE SEEK TO HELP ITSELF? 

Our people, faced with the danger of being completely destroyed by Hitler, did many things to try to save its life as we shall sec. But once Hitler plunged the world into war, one thing became more important than any other: to help defeat him and his Fascist forces.

JEWISH FAITH IN VICTORY 

Our people helped the democratic nations along the road to victory in many ways. The first was to help strengthen the courage to fight back. World War I had brought so much destruction and had left the peoples so sick of war that many leaders were willing to do almost anything rather than fight. They foolishly imagined that if they would only give in a little to a tyrant like Hitler, and let him do what he wanted to the Jews and the democratic parties in Germany, and then to Austria and to Czechoslovakia, he would be satisfied and they would not have to fight him. But wise statesmen and great religious leaders warned their countries that anyone who breaks God's laws in one place will go on to do it in another. They warned, "If you allow terror and force to crush other peoples, they will end up by crushing us, too. Jewish leaders were among the most active in this warning. 11 Besides, many people were afraid of Hitler. He had made them believe, through speeches and movies that his armies could never be beaten, that his planes and tanks and hardened soldiers could crush anyone. He said that the Democracies, especially, would quickly fall before him, because they were trained in the weak and sickly ideas of mercy and kindness, while the Nazis were trained in the manly idea that the strong must rule and show no mercy.

Even an American writer called Fascism "The Wave of the Future. By this she meant that it was certain to rise up and overcome other forms of government such as Democracy. 

Courageous people, however, still believed that right would win over might. They refused to be afraid, even though Hitler did have the greatest army that the world had ever seen. The 

Jews were among them. Our people remembered our own struggles for freedom against Pharaoh's armies of horsemen. They remembered how, later on, the Maccabees fought off the greatest army of their day: Antiochus' armored soldiers. When one of the Jewish warriors then had trembled at the sight of the enemy and had said, "How shall we, being few, be able to fight against so great a multitude, and so strong?" Judah answered: It is an easy matter for many to be conquered by the hands of a few; and there is no difference in the sight of the God of heaven to deliver with a great multitude, or with a small company. For the success of war is not in the multitude of the army; but strength cometh from heaven. They come against us with brutal multitude and pride, to destroy us and our wives and our children and to take

86. our spoils. But we fight for our lives and our laws. And the 

Lord himself will overthrow them before our face; fear them not," (I Maccabees 3)

This is the type of courage that the Jews added to the courage of the fearless peoples who refused to let Hitler simply frighten them out of their freedom. The men of courage forced their leaders to resist Hitler and to fight back when he invaded more and more countries.

JEWISH SERVICE IN THE ARMED FORCES 

The same spirit strengthened the Jewish soldiers and sailors as they did their part in the battles against the Axis all over the world. World War II called forth the greatest heroism from many nations who struggled against military power much greater than their own. The world will long remember the courage and skill of the British as they saved their nation from defeat by rescuing their soldiers from the beaches at Dunkirk after France had fallen and by fighting off the Nazi blitz (bombing raids) night after night with R.A.F. pilots who were red-eyed from lack of sleep. It will remember the poorly-equipped Greeks who were driving out their Italian invaders until the Nazis came in to crush them, and the Yugoslavs who refused to accept defeat and continued to fight on with great power from their secret camps in the mountains. It will remember the long struggle of the Russians whose most valuable industrial cities and farms were captured by the Nazis, but who rebuilt their factories and armies with great will-power and became the first to stop the Nazi machine and to drive it back. The world will remember the long, unyielding fight of the Chinese against Japan. 

In all these but the last (for there are almost no Jews in China), the Jewish men were among the heroes. Thirty Jews were among those honored by the British for their courage at Dunkirk. 

Three of the first thirteen men to receive the George Cross, highest decoration for unusual heroism, were Jews. Col. Mordecai 

Frizi was the first Greek officer of field rank to fall for his country; the Archbishop of Athens presided at his funeral. Over sixteen thousand Jews, not merely from Yugoslavia, but from Bulgaria, Greece, and Hungary served as fighters and officers in the daring Yugoslav guerrilla armies. They had their own rabbinical chaplains and synagogue services. As they would swoop down upon 

Nazi concentration camps and free Jews held there, the latter 

Would immediately join the guerrilla forces.

Jewish service with the Russian army has also been upon a heroic level. The Russian air force which finally stopped the 

Nazi Luftwaffe was commanded by General Jacob Shmushkevitch, who won the most desired honor: "Hero of the Soviet Union. So did 

General Kreiser, who worked his army out of a Nazi trap near Smolensk, and General Lev Dovator, who was killed leading his soldiers in the recapture of Rostov. General Cherniakovsky was the leader of one of the great armies in the invasion of Germany. 

Dozens of other Jews likewise served as Soviet generals. Sub-

87. marine Commander Israel Fisanowitch was twice decorated as "Hero" for sinking eight Nazi transports.

Behind the front, Jewish engineers did unusual service. won the great "Hero" award for organizing tank production, and six others won the "Order of the Red Star" for increasing the production of fighter planes and bombers. Two

Jews of other defeated countries joined with their courageous fellow citizens to continue the fight. After the defeat of Poland, the Jews (who had been only 10 per cent of the Polish population) formed 12 per cent of the Polish troops fighting in France. When France went down, Jews formed 15 per cent of the 

Polish troops fighting in Russia. Thousands more joined the 

Czech, Greek, French, and Dutch legions.

A mighty contribution toward the defeat of Hitler came from the Jews of Palestine. That Jewish community knew that the war must be won or the Jewish people would die. Therefore, at the very beginning of the war, it made up a list of 136,000 ablebodied men and women (one-fourth of the whole population) who volunteered to fight. The Jewish Agency offered to organize these men and women into a Jewish army under its own flag, to serve under British commanders. The British refused the offer on the ground that it might displease the Arabs. It did allow a small number of Jews to enlist in separate Jewish units in the regular British forces, provided there were an equal number of Arab volunteers. Later it allowed more Jews to enlist even though there was no equal number of Arabs willing to fight and formed a Jewish Brigade.

The thousands of Palestine Jews who did serve (30,000 in January, 1944) helped in the hardest battles. They fought in 

France and at Dunkirk; they covered the retreats in Greece and 

Crete. Of the 10,000 British troops missing in those two lands, 1,023 were Jews of Palestine. When the R.A.F. ground crews had to be transferred to Britain after Dunkirk, 1,500 Jewish mechanics and specialists from the Holy Land took over their work. Palestinian Jews made up three-fifths of the guerrilla experts whose silent raids destroyed Italian forts in Abyssinia night after night and led to the final Italian surrender there. Fifty 

Chalutsim of northern Palestine guided the Australians in their attack which took Syria away from the control of Vichy France and its master, Hitler. The Ministry of Information in London declared that the capture of Tobruk would have been impossible without the aid of Palestine volunteers. Later, in 1942, when 

General Rommel's drive into Egypt threatened the whole Near East, a London paper revealed that the defense works which served to stop him just before Alexandria "were 100 per cent the work of Palestine Jews - officers and men. Palestine Jews thus helped make possible the great turning point of the war in the Mediterranean. A Jewish transport division was given special praise for its great role in General Alexander's sweeping counter-attack which was the basis for driving the Axis entirely out of Africa. 

General Frederick Kisch of Palestine later killed in action

88. was given a special honor by the king for distinguished service as Senior Engineer of the Eighth Army.

Jewish Palestine also served as a most necessary source of military supplies. Over twelve hundred factories produced materials for war, including clothing and needed electrical and automobile parts. The Palestine Potash works became the chief source of this valuable chemical for the entire British Empire. 

The Hebrew University provided serums to cure typhoid fever and other diseases of that area. This university also furnished weather information, and measuring and medical instruments. 

Skilled scientists, driven out of Europe to Palestine by the Nazis because they were Jews, produced valuable drugs, optical instruments, and diamonds for the industries of the United Nations. help this work in the summer of 1942 - when the Nazis were at the gates of Alexandria the Chief Rabbis and members of the Rabbinical Court gave permission for even the most Orthodox Jew to work on Saturdays, since, "no sacrifice is too great to speed up the war effort for victory. 

JEWS IN THE U.S. WAR EFFORT 

To

Every Jew who lived in the United States in the exciting years of the war, was proud of the contribution of great heroes which his people provided. Most popular of them all was Sergeant 

Meyer Levin. He flashed to fame as the bombardier who sent his bombs straight to their mark in Capt. Colin Kelly's famous successful attack on the Japanese battle-ship Haruna. In the battle of the Coral Sea, Sgt. Levin scored again, probably sinking a 15,000 ton transport. His continuous record of expert marksmanship won him three major honors, including the Distinguished Flying Cross. In January, 1943, he volunteered to go with a flight, even though he had not been ordered to go, in order to give his expert help in recognizing enemy ships. The plane was forced down at sea and he was last seen unhooking the life rafts which saved his companions' lives. 

Evidence of Jewish heroism in this war began from the day 

America was attacked at Pearl Harbor. Here Ensign Ira Weil

Jeffrey died in action keeping his ammunition line going on the U.S.S. California. A destroyer was later named to honor his memory. Stanley Caplan, an Ensign with little training, took a destroyer to sea with but three other young officers, when their commanders had been killed or wounded. They helped shoot down four Jap planes and depth bomb two submarines. Lt. Com. Solomon 

Isquith received the Navy Cross for rescuing 90 per cent of his men from the sunken target ship, Utah. On the Philippines, where his father had served many years before, Lt. Henry Mark was killed throwing grenades at a Jap tank. His bravery won him the

Distinguished Service Cross. Radioman David Goodman of Lt. Com. 

Bulkeley's mosquito boat squadron, won honors for helping to bring Gen. MacArthur out of Corregidor and for his aid in torpedoing a Jap cruiser. He was later captured by the Japanese.

89. 

Medical Aid Hymie Epstein. 

In the sniper-filled jungle battles of New Guinea, he refused to consider his own safety and crawled right to the front, time and again, to bind up the wounded and bring them back. On his last trip the injured man warned him not to come because of the hail of bullets. Epstein came on, saved the many but could not get back himself. Sgt. Barney Ross, former light-weight and welter-weight boxing champion of the world, showed his sportsmanship when he refused to leave when his unit retreated from a position far ahead of the rest of their line. Instead, he stood guard over three wounded man through the night and saved them by firing five hundred rounds of ammunition to keep off Jap snipers.

CHAPLAINS 

The U. S. Governmant recognized that men in this hard but just and necessary war would need deep religious faith. There fore, it distributed several million copies of the Bible. It included a foreword in which President Roosevelt called it a "fountain of strength." The government asked that many more chaplains volunteer than in World War I. Rabbis responded well, as did the leaders of other faiths. By May, 1944, there were 

241 rabbis serving as chaplains, including thirty-one in the

Navy. Ninety-five were already overseas or en route. (In World 

War I there were only twenty-six Jewish chaplains altogether.)

The chaplains provided religious worship and teaching for the men and acted as their friends and advisers. They helped them to solve their problems at camp and back home. They built friendship among Protestants, Catholics, and Jews, fighting and working together in the great cause. Chaplains were not called "rabbi, or "minister, or "priest, but only "chaplain. The government had taught all of them to be able to conduct the services and the ceremonies of the other faiths, as well as their own. Thus any chaplain could help any man if a leader of his own faith was not available. 11 11 #.. 

A striking illustration of the spirit of the chaplaincy came in the heroism of four chaplains - two ministers, one priest, and one rabbi: Alexander Goode, who graduated from the Hebrew Union

College in 1935. When the transport upon which they were crossing the North Atlantic was torpedoed early in 1943, the four religious leaders gave their life-preservers to service men who had none and were last seen praying together on the deck of the sinking ship.

CIVILIAN SERVICES 

The Jewish Welfare Board had been organized in 1917 to provide religious services and club-rooms for Jewish fighting men at that time. It now set up an even larger program to meet the needs of World War II. In cooperation with the organizations of rabbis, it took charge of the activities of the Jewish chaplains. 

It prepared Jewish prayer books, calendar-diaries, an abridged Bible, and booklets on Jewish history and religion. It joined together with Protestant and Catholic organizations to form the

90. 

U.S.O. ( (United Service Organizations). The U.S.O. set up hundreds of club-rooms just outside of the military camps, where reading rooms, entertainment, dances, and refreshments were provided. 

The J.W.B. civilian workers who directed some of these clubs secured the help of near-by synagogues and Jewish organizations to conduct Passover Seder services and other special events for the men. In Jewish communities which were not near camps, the 

J.W.B. organized "Serve-a-Camp committees to provide gift-boxes, magazines, Purim goodies, records, phonographs, and other things for U.S.O. clubs and their men.

Every Jewish organization in America did its part in the giant effort to keep up the faith and spirit of the young Jewish people in the service. Besides cooperating with the J.W.B., many made special plans of their own. One plan was the B'nai B'rith's "Adopt-a-Ship" program, by which a lodge would provide a radio, books, records, and the like for a particular ship of the Navy, and hold a party for the sailors whenever they were in port. 

American Jews likewise did their part on the home front. They enlisted as air-raid wardens, plane spotters, volunteer fire fighters, Red Cross workers, leaders of salvage drives, and blood donors. Successful War Bond campaigns were conducted by almost all Jewish groups, including Temples, the Zionist Organizations, and the Jewish War Veterans. 11 In the darkest days just before the war, when Hitlerism was growing and threatening to sweep everything before it, and nations seemed too weak to resist, Irving Berlin lifted the spirit of our country with his song, "God Bless America. It was sung as a hymn of praise to God "for a land that's free." Then, when the war came, Mr. Berlin, who had cheered America's soldiers during the last war with his musical shows, took up that task again. 

He wrote and produced "This Is the Army, which not only delighted the men in uniform but earned millions of dollars from civilian performances. This money Mr. Berlin donated to the Army Emergency Relief Fund, to care for the families of soldiers which were suffering hardships because of the war. 

WAR PRODUCTION 

Jews also did their part in speeding the production of war materials which turned the victory toward our side. Sidney Hillman, experienced labor leader, was appointed by the President in 

January, 1941 as Associate Director General of the Office of Production Management, and Director of its Labor Division. His task was to retrain hundreds of thousands of workers and transfer them into defense industries. 

Bernard M. Baruch, who had been wonderfully successful in raising American production in 1918 as Chairman of the War Industries Board, was called upon time and again for advice in the production problems of World War II. He made studies of vitally needed materials, such as rubber. In 1943, he was appointed to head a War and Post-War Advisory Unit under the Director of War 

Mobilization.

Here he helped to plan ways to turn war industries

91. back to peace-time work after the war, as quickly as possible, so that men would not be unemployed. In connection with our rubber production program the advice given by Dr. Chaim Weizmann was publicly acknowledged by the Vice-President of the United States 

Henry A. Wallace. 

Justice Samuel I. Rosenman of the New York State Supreme 

Court, long a close friend and adviser of President Roosevelt, was called by him in September, 1943, to become the President's official legal adviser. 

The heroic efforts of Christians and Jews working together in America, and of men of many faiths and many nations throughout the world working hand in hand for freedom proved irresistible, even against the might of Hitler. And as the United Nations forces advanced and rescued from Nazi torture and death communities with large Jewish populations, their wild joy knew no bounds. 

A reporter who entered the Jewish quarter of Tripoli with the British army described how the Jewish people there "threw themselves at our feet, offering gifts of not only wine and tobacco, but money as well. Men threw their arms about our necks and kissed our cheeks. Women kissed our hands. Children clung to our legs and kissed our dusty shoes. You simply cannot grasp the effect that this victory had on the people, until you try to wade through the streets with little children clinging to your feet and beseeching you, not for material things, but for a word, or a smile of comfort. The leaders told us that a few hours before the troops had entered the city, Jewish merchants had been dragged from their shops and shot. 

No less thrilling was the deliverance of 1,400 Jews in an

Italian concentration camp early in 1944. One day they looked up to behold trucks and men carrying a Mogen Dovid coming to liberate them. These were Palestinian soldiers serving in the five Jewish companies on the Italian front. Some of them found their own relatives among the rescued one, his sister, and an 

American Jewish boy, his own father! 

CARED FOR THE SUFFERING 

Hitler had to be driven out. Yet it was clear that this would not, by itself, solve all the problems of the Jewish people. 

In Tripoli, Italy, Russia, and everywhere, rescued Jews had been so stripped of their money and belongings by the anti-Semitic laws and by Nazi thieves that they needed clothes, food, and shelter at once. Many had no businesses or jobs to go back to. 

Work had to be found for them. Many would have to be trained for new and different kinds of work than they had ever done before. 

Many would have to begin life all over again in a new and more favorable land.

The Jewish people throughout the world had been trying to help their suffering brothers in these ways all through the difficult years between the wars. The first method they used was to send money for food, warm clothes, and medicine just to keep them

92. alive until conditions could be made better. To do this, various groups doing relief work, as this is called, joined together to form the American Jewish Joint Distribution Committee (J.D.C.) in 

1914. The terrible suffering of the Jews in Eastern Europe, along the Russo-German battle line, gave the J.D.C. the largest relief task up to that time in Jewish history. American Jews gave most generously; Julius Rosenwald gave a million dollars, in the critical year of 1918, as one-tenth of the country's quota. 

In its first ten years (1915-24), the J.D.C. spent $60,000,000 keeping our people alive in Eastern Europe. German, English, and 

French Jewish organizations joined in the charitable work. 

The need for relief then declined until Fascism began to rise in Poland, Rumania, and Germany. Then in the first ten years of Hitler's rule (1933-43), the J.D.C. spent $40,000,000. 

When war threatened to break out, the J.D.C. feared that its help would be cut off, since money cannot be sent readily from one country into an enemy land. Therefore it set up separate funds in those countries. These continued to give help even after the war came. Indeed, after Poland was crushed by Germany, J.D.C. soup kitchens were feeding and keeping alive as many as one-half million people a day.

AIDED BUSINESS AND RETRAINED WORKERS 

At- But it was not enough to just keep people on relief. tempts were therefore made to help them to be able to support themselves again. In Poland, the J.D.C. set up a huge Loan-Fund. 

Jewish men who had lost their business especially through boycott in Poland, could borrow money from it and establish new shops or be loaned to others who were in need. 

A very effective means of helping Jews to support themselves was to retrain them for industry and agriculture. We have learned above (page 59) how, following the revolution in Russia, the Agro-Joint and the ICA assisted in this work, especially in agriculture. The ORT (organization for rehabilitation and training) was the great agency teaching Jews to work in factories. 

Founded in Russia in 1880 to prepare the Jews for the coming of industries, its large-scale work began during World War I when it retrained and found new work for 25,000 homeless Jews. After the war its efforts were enlarged to help the "declassed" Jews of Russia. A World ORT was then formed to retrain Jews suffering from depressions and boycotts in Poland, Rumania, and elsewhere. 

ORT set up trade schools and workshops, cooperative farms and farm schools. It

It provided great quantities of tools and machinery for the newly-trained workers and sometimes raw materials such as cloth, to get them started in their manufacturing. 

When the Nazis took over Germany in 1933, Jews began to flee from that country. It soon became clear that other countries did not want many merchants or doctors or lawyers to come in, but would take certain numbers of trained farmers and factory workers. 

Therefore, ORT set up training schools for these occupations in

93. Germany and in lands to which refugees had fled England, France, Switzerland, China, Canada, and Argentina. Today ORT has trade schools for Jews in other lands, including one in the United States. Everywhere, it takes Jews off charity roles and makes them self-supporting citizens.

IMMIGRATION 

As time went on, it became clearer and clearer that in the lands upon which the curse of hatred had fallen, the Jews would not be happy no matter how much food and clothing were sent in, or how well they would be retrained. Therefore more and more sought a new life in lands of freedom. 

The chief countries which received these refugees were Palestine, the United States, and the Latin American Republics. 

From 1933 through 1941, Palestine became the new home of 212,000 

Jews; the United States, 153,000; South America, about 60,000.

THE UNITED STATES 

Before World War I, the United States had been the world's great haven of refuge to which all oppressed or poverty-stricken peoples could flee. The country had welcomed masses of immigrants who sometimes came in at the rate of a million a year, helping to build the country in every way. Many Jews had been among these; over two million between 1881 and 1914. 

The destructive war, however, brought the fear that there might be too great an immigration from the suffering lands of Europe. At the same time, certain people believed that the 

United States should always continue to be of mostly West European stock (English, German, Irish, French, etc.) and wanted to cut down on the more recent large immigrations from Eastern and 

Southern Europe (Poland, Russia, Greece, Italy, etc.). Therefore a series of laws were passed, beginning in 1921, which greatly reduced immigration and put it on a quota system for each country. 

With the coming of the business depression in 1930, President Hoover ordered American consuls to reduce the number of newcomers even further, for fear that they might add to the millions of 

Americans without jobs.

When Hitlerism made it desperately necessary for large numbers of liberals and Jews to flee for their lives, pleas were sent to the government to admit more immigrants. By 1937 and 

1938, with better business conditions, President Roosevelt allowed consuls abroad to be more generous. By 1939, almost the full quota of immigrants from oppressed countries was allowed to come in. Forty per cent of the people saved in these more liberal years (1937-41) were Jews.

American Jewry guaranteed that these newcomers would not have to be supported by charity. Everyone who was admitted had an affidavit from a responsible American citizen with money in the bank, who guaranteed to take care of him should he ever need

AC

94. financial help. To make sure that the refugees would not need help, a National Refugee Service was formed. It assisted the newcomers, meeting them at the boat, and providing those who needed it with temporary homes, money, and medical care. It sent as many of them as possible out of crowded New York into other Jewish communities which had promised to find jobs for them or set them up in business. It provided schooling in the English language and in American customs, and trained many in new types of work to make it easier for them to secure employment. 

Because of this wise planning, and because the newcomers were exceptionally well educated and industrious people, they soon became a help to America. Instead of increasing the number of unemployed, they decreased it by setting up new businesses. 

Some even produced articles which, before this time, America had had to import from abroad.

Refugee professors, including Albert Einstein, added their knowledge to American institutions of learning. Refugee artists and writers added to American cultural enjoyment: authors such as 

Lion Feuchtwanger, and dramatic producers, such as Max Reinhardt. 

REFUGEES AIDED WAR PRODUCTION 

With the coming of the war in America, the refugees showed even more strikingly the benefits they brought to this land. 

Among them were 5,000 physicians, Christian and Jewish, who helped greatly to relieve the shortage of medical men in civilian life, created by the needs of the armed forces. Others were electrical, aeronautical, mining, and other types of engineers: required in war production. Prof. Albert Einstein, greatest scientist of our age, conducted special research for the U.S. 

Navy to make American explosives more effective. Many refugees possessed a knowledge of the European languages and some knew even the less familiar tongues, such as Japanese. Many had an intimate knowledge of the industries, plants, business, and social conditions of enemy countries. One had even been manager of the Junkers Airline System in Europe, another of the Fiat works of Italy. Such knowledge of languages and conditions could be put to valuable military use in a "global war. Several thousand young refugees served in the armed forces, many enlisting even before Pearl Harbor.

As in Palestine and in Great Britain, where seventy thousand refugees have worked long hours with great skill and eagerness to produce munitions and needed goods, so in America, the exiles driven out by Hitler have been his loss and the great gain of the 

United Nations.

LATIN AMERICA 

The Latin American countries did their share to provide new homes for refugees during the terrible Hitler years. In these fertile lands of vast natural resources, small populations, and unoccupied areas, new and hopeful Jewish communities were begun.

95. 

Three-fourths of Latin America's 400,000 Jews lived in Argentina, whose capital city of Buenos Aires, with its suburbs, contained a strong Jewry of 175,000.

Baron Maurice de Hirsch of Germany had long before laid the basis for this immigration. After his building of a railroad from the Balkans to Constantinople had brought him enormous wealth, he determined to use it to help his suffering fellow Jews of Eastern Europe. In 1891, therefore, he created the Jewish 

Colonization Association (ICA) and gave it $10,000,000. Later, at his death, he left it $30,000,000 Its purpose was to get the Jews out of Russia and settle them on farms in Argentina, where he bought a million and a half fertile acres. He loaned money to the Jewish farmers to build homes and buy equipment; and they gradually paid it back. Baron de Hirsch had hoped to transfer great masses there. But in the first ten years only 10,000 went there. Many of these later left the land and went into the cities. A beginning, however, had been made and today one-tenth of Argentina Jewry still lives on the De Hirsch agricultural settlements.

From 1933 through 1941, Argentina admitted 25,000 Jewish refugees. In the same period, Brazil admitted almost as many, and Uruguay, 10,000.

97. IX.

WORKING FOR A JEWISH HOMELAND IN PALESTINE 

It was Palestine that was able to save the greatest number of Jews from Hitlerism. This was because it was the one place in the world which had been planned from the beginning to take care of Jewry in time of need. Its purpose had been to give the 

Jew one land in which he could live 'as of right and not on sufferance, that is, one land in which he could live because he chose to, without asking the permission of anyone else. This control over his own life in Palestine had not yet been fully won when Hitler arose. The British government under the League of Nations mandates system still controlled the most important matters of immigration and purchase of land. 

But our people was clearly moving toward control over its own fate, as shown by the fact that in Palestine it had the power to force open the gates to Jews who had to be rescued, even though others objected. Here Jews could insist that they had a right to come in. In other lands they had no such right and had to accept whatever hospitality was, or was not, offered to them. 

For this reason, the millions of Jews in the great and rich United States could provide haven from Hitlerism for only one refugee for every thirty Jews already in the country. But in 

Palestine, a tiny and comparatively poor land, rescue was provided during this period for many more Jews than had lived there before (186,000 had lived there in 1932; 212,000 were rescued from 1933-41).

Here is how it happened: After the Arab riots of 1929 and the decision practically to stop the upbuilding of Jewish Palestine (see pages 37-38), the Jewish leaders resigned. They declared that Britain had not kept its promise in the Balfour Declaration. The British gave in somewhat and a few thousand immigrants were admitted each year. But as the Jewish situation in Poland grew worse and Hitler came to power in Germany, the demand of the Jewish people that Palestine carry out its true purpose as a homeland could not be refused. In 1932, 9,500 Jews entered Palestine; in 1933, 30,000; in 1934, 42,000; in 1935, 62,000! All the arguments of the British and Arabs that the land did not have the "economic absorptive capacity" (the ability of the land to absorb refugees and to sustain them) for large immigration were shown to be false. The large numbers of newcomers did not bring unemployment, but a great wave of prosperity, building and progress to the land. 

NEW LIFE AND CONFIDENCE 

Great new cities were built, clean, shining in the sun. 

Tel-Aviv (which means "Hill of Spring") had been founded by sixty families in 1909. By 1937, it had grown into a thriving modern metropolis of 150,000. In the countryside, healthy, hard-working and attractive colonies popped up all over. Using scientific farming, Jewish workers produced 300 pounds of wheat per dunam (quarter of an acre) instead of the Arab average of fifty pounds.

· 98.. 

Jews bred chickens. that produced 150 eggs a year instead of the seventy produced by the old varieties. They introduced new 

European breeds of cows which yielded up to 4,500quarts of milk a year in contrast with the 900 quarts yielded by the Arab cows. 

Millions of cases of citrus fruits were shipped to war countries, where the very name, "Jaffa," meant the best of all the oranges.

Industries and factories of all kinds were built and enlarged by the skill and money of the newcomers. Electric power, generated by the river Jordan, turned the wheels of the factories, pasteurized the milk and lit the houses and theaters of the country. 

But even more thrilling than all of these was the spirit of freedom and the new confidence in themselves which the immigrants breathed in with the very air. In Germany and elsewhere they had been made fun of, insulted, tormented, and even tortured because they were Jews. The children were lined up in school and were pointed out to the other children as liars and cheats by nature. 

Year after year of such treatment had injured and crushed their spirits. Many had begun to wonder whether there was not really something wrong with themselves and with the Jewish people, if others treated them so. But in Palestine, they saw at once that far from being a despised and inferior race, their people was among the greatest creative peoples; they could turn barren deserts into blooming orange groves, and deadly malarial swamps into healthy villages. And this they did as Jews, by the power of their own hands and their own skill. 

In Palestine, also, these newcomers with crushed spirits saw that Jews could sing again their own songs in their own language. They saw that Jews could produce the finest music, plays, poetry, and novels. At Sukos time, they saw courtyards beckoning with booths of fruit and bowers. At Chanuko time, they saw hillsides lit up with Menorahs. At Shovuos, they saw the 

Emek alive with processions of thank-offerings to God. On the 

Sabbath, they felt a peace and neighborly feeling descend upon the whole land, where all businesses ceased and synagogues and cultural assemblies rallied the people to proclaim that "God is One" and "Israel is One. Here the Jew was revealing to himself and to the world the true power and grandeur of the Jewish faith, of the Jewish will-to-live, of the Hebrew language, and the culture of Israel. 11 Here was the surest healing for Jewish spirits torn by the awful torments of Hitlerism. Elsewhere, even in the freest lands, newcomers were considered "refugees, strangers helped by charity. In Palestine there are no "refugees. All are newcomers. All, therefore, are equally welcome, as Jews seeking to rebuild their land and their faith in themselves. 

That is why among the Jews of Hitlerized Europe more sought to go to Palestine than anywhere else. When they could not get permission from the British to enter, they came in secretly from across the desert, or swam in from little-boats along the shore at night. Anything to get to Palestine. There they would be safe; their own people would protect them and clothe them.
